### Page 2

Hungary: Market sentiment expected to
keep NBH in wait-and-see mode
Tuesday brings the National Bank of Hungary's latest interest rate
decision. While much of the world is grappling with renewed
inﬂation fears, Hungary remains something of an island of safety,
with both headline and core inﬂation hovering around 2% year-on-
year. That gives the NBH room to ease – but the door remains
locked, and global market sentiment holds the key. With markets

increasingly pricing aggressive rate hikes from major central banks
and market tensions elevated, the NBH has little choice but to
remain in wait-and-see mode. The risk is that by the time the
external backdrop structurally improves, domestic inﬂationary
pressures may already have started to close the window for cuts.
For now, low inﬂation argues for easing, but global markets are
forcing patience. In our base case, we see the base rate remaining

at 5.50% for at least the next three quarters.
Wage data for September will be released on Wednesday. Wage
growth will likely remain elevated for now, but the days of
exceptionally strong real wage growth are numbered. Employers
and employees are negotiating a new minimum wage deal for
2027, as the existing three-year agreement would imply an
unsustainably high 14% increase. We expect the new deal to deliver
a rise of around 5%, setting the stage for a meaningful slowdown in

overall wage growth and easing one of the key sources of domestic
inﬂationary pressure.
Czech Republic: Producer prices expected
to have climbed in October
Czech PPI data will be released on Monday. Producer prices likely
continued their monthly gains in October, which have driven the
annual growth pace higher. Both the surging Brent crude price and
depreciating koruna contributed to rising input costs that were only
partially passed to end prices, putting proﬁtability under extended

pressure.

Document 342: FX Outlook 2027_ Seven key risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Outlook 2027_ Seven key risks.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

Low volatility masks a wide range of tail risks for 2027. AI stress,
another inﬂation shock, ﬁscal concerns, elections and
geopolitical escalation could all trigger sharp FX moves, while
ﬁnancial repression and a carry unwind add further asymmetric
risks

Chris Turner and Francesco Pesole
AI bust: expect unorthodox FX reactions
The resilience of the AI trade in equities has been tested of late, and
a more decisive unwind could become an overarching FX driver next
year. We think the impact of a major US tech selloﬀ on currencies
would depend on how far it spreads to broader US and global risk
assets, whether it heavily threatens ﬁnancial stability, and how it
impacts the inﬂation backdrop. Crucially, the initial FX reaction may
look very diﬀerent from the medium-term move.
The dollar still retains decent safe-haven appeal by our estimates,
having recovered from the 2025 “sell America” period. Yet episodes
in 2026 where AI concerns hit tech stocks without materially

### Page 2

spilling into broader equities were accompanied by some USD
underperformance. We think contained US tech-speciﬁc weakness
would continue to weigh moderately on the dollar next year, with
low-yielding haven currencies such as the yen, Swiss franc and euro
beneﬁting the most.
The dynamics would be diﬀerent if tech losses triggered a broader
selloﬀ. In that scenario, we would expect an initial safe-haven boost
to the dollar, followed by a larger depreciation as the shock

deepens and the Federal Reserve response comes into focus. The
greater the threat to ﬁnancial stability, the stronger the case for the
Fed to provide liquidity and prevent a global ﬁnancial crisis-style
credit crunch, ultimately weighing on the dollar. Over the medium
term, we would expect JPY, CHF and EUR to outperform, while the
Australian dollar, New Zealand dollar and non-European EM
currencies would be hit harder, consistent with recent AI-driven

selloﬀs (see chart below).
There is, however, an important inﬂation caveat. A severe risk-oﬀ
shock would itself reduce inﬂation risks through potential job
losses, negative wealth eﬀects and tighter credit conditions. But if
inﬂation remains elevated, the Fed may be slower to ease, not least
to preserve the safe-haven role of the Treasury market. In that
scenario, we think the dollar would outperform for longer, while
European currencies would be less insulated than under a more

benign inﬂation backdrop.

currencies

### Page 3

Asian currencies have been excluded due to time-zone mismatch
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
A new 2022 for inﬂation
Our baseline assumes contained second-round eﬀects from the
energy shock, allowing major European central banks to cut rates
towards the end of 2027. Four risks could challenge that view.
Second-round eﬀects may simply take longer to emerge, as a
prolonged crisis and higher gas prices erode corporate buﬀers and
feed into food and services inﬂation. Tighter labour markets could

amplify the shock through stronger wage growth, while renewed
Middle East escalation could drive another sharp rise in energy
prices. Finally, greater ﬁscal support could cushion the hit to
growth, but at the cost of higher medium-term inﬂation pressures.
In such a scenario, we would expect broad dollar gains. Historically,
low-yielders have tended to suﬀer most when Fed tightening drives
global yields higher, but we would be wary of applying that

playbook mechanically in 2027. The relative central bank response
should be the key diﬀerentiator, with those that have tightened less
so far retaining more ammunition to defend their currencies.
We still see the Norwegian krone and Swedish krona as particularly
vulnerable given their low liquidity and high beta, while AUD could
be a standout loser given the Reserve Bank of Australia's limited
room to tighten further amid domestic vulnerabilities. EUR and JPY,

by contrast, could prove more resilient than in 2022, provided the

European Central Bank and Bank of Japan respond swiftly to

Current central bank rates vs latest peak
Source: ING, Macrobond
Fiscal takeover: contained, but
potentially explosive risk
We’ve all seen the French bond sell-oﬀ unfold over recent
months. The heart of the story is one of budget credibility. If
investors suspect that a government will not or cannot amend a
worrying budget trajectory, then they will demand higher

coupons or, in extremis, as in Greece 2010, refuse to buy
government debt. France is no Greece and our base case
assumes that some modest budget consolidation can keep
French spreads contained in 2027. Even if French politicians fail
to address investor concerns, the ECB’s Transmission Protection
Instrument (TPI) backstop can prevent broader contagion.
What about the US? One area of concern is that near 60% of US
Treasury issuance is in the form of short-term bill issuance, as

well as its large reﬁnancing requirements. What happens if
inﬂation spikes and the US Treasury has to pay ever-higher
reﬁnancing rates? Equally, what happens if White House policy
again upends the dollar-US Treasury safe-haven correlation as
was the case in March/April 2025? As we saw at the time, the
breakdown in these correlations can trigger some of the most

### Page 5

extreme dollar selling. And the fact that investors are again
running low FX hedge ratios on US assets warns of a similar

Debt ﬁnancing overview
Source: IMF, ING
Watch the EU election calendar!
2027 will be a busy year for eurozone political risk, with markets
focused primarily on ﬁscal implications and the threat of EU
political fragmentation. Expect any negative EUR impact to make
the Swiss franc a standout outperformer, thanks to its European

safe-haven role.
French elections combine both ﬁscal and EU-wide risks and are
likely to matter most. The biggest downside for the euro would
come from a surprise victory by Jean-Luc Mélenchon in the April-
May presidential election or a lack of a clear majority in potential
parliamentary elections over the summer. To a lesser extent, a
Marine Le Pen victory could also weigh on sentiment if early signals
point to a more confrontational approach towards the EU than

already expected.
In Germany, several state elections will be watched for signs of
greater government instability or further gains by the Eurosceptic
Alternative for Germany (AfD). Markets will focus particularly on the
implications for Germany’s reform agenda, and by extension the
growth outlook.

### Page 6

Italian elections rank third among our political risks. Although
scheduled by December, a May-June vote looks increasingly likely.
Prime Minister Giorgia Meloni remains favoured to secure another
mandate, potentially backed by a broader coalition. Italian asset
resilience has relied on Meloni’s ﬁscal discipline, so a surprise
victory by the centre-left bloc would, in our view, pose material
downside risks to BTPs and the euro.
A busy election calendar for next year

Source: ING, average of pollsters
Debasement trade strikes back
2025 was the year of dollar debasement, when fears over a
captured Fed led to pressure on the dollar against the Swiss franc,
while bitcoin and gold went bid. We had a ﬂash of that trade again
this August, when Scott Bessent’s extension of longer-dated US
Treasury buybacks unnerved the bond market and USD/CHF was hit
hard.
Were US Treasury yields to rise sharply next year, the theme of

‘ﬁnancial repression’ could re-emerge. Here the US Treasury could
try to contain Treasury yields by providing regulatory incentives to
banks to hold more Treasuries or put pressure on the Fed to
intervene. Any suggestion that the Treasury would not be allowing

USD is an efﬁcient safe-haven, but things

Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
The end of the carry trade?
Popular carry trade target currencies through 2026 have again
been the Turkish lira and the Nigerian naira. On the former, Turkish
presidential and parliamentary elections must be held by May
2028 and present some risk that the Central Bank of Turkey may

opt to change its policy of a controlled depreciation in the lira. That
would come as a big shock to the market. Nigeria looks slightly
better positioned after recent reforms to fuel subsidies and in the
energy sector.
When looking at the popular funding currencies of the yen, we
doubt slightly more aggressive BoJ tightening would be enough to
trigger a big short squeeze in the yen. And speculators are actually
running net long yen positions today as opposed to the large short

yen positions held in summer 2024 – the last time we had a major
adjustment in yen positioning.
The biggest driver of a carry trade unwind would probably come
through a ﬁnancial shock. Were any unwind in the AI trade to
uncover stresses or imbalances in, for example, the private credit
area and lead to ﬁnancial distress, volatility would surge and value-

### Page 8

at-risk considerations could prompt indiscriminate deleveraging.
We haven’t really seen much ﬁnancial distress since the Silicon
Valley Bank crisis of 2023.
A big carry trade unwind would be yen and Swiss franc positive,
while negative for the high-yielding activity currencies.
Geopolitical blowout: more than one
front to watch
There are three major fronts to watch in terms of geopolitical tail
risks next year. All of these would see the dollar as the big

outperformer.
A spillover of Russia’s war beyond Ukraine, or a material escalation
in cyber and hybrid attacks on European countries, would hit
European currencies the hardest. CEE currencies and the
Scandinavian bloc would be particularly exposed given their
geographical proximity and sensitivity to regional security risks.
The Swiss franc and yen would join the dollar in a big rally.
A broader Middle East conﬂict drawing in additional regional or

external powers would mainly hit FX through higher energy prices.
Net energy importers would suﬀer most, particularly currencies
where central banks have limited scope for additional tightening to
oﬀset the inﬂationary shock. Here, the dollar would be a clear
winner, as other low-yielders would suﬀer from the energy shock.
A sharp escalation in geopolitical tensions in East Asia would put
Asian currencies under heavy pressure, with AUD and NZD also

vulnerable as liquid proxies. European currencies would be relatively
more insulated from the initial shock. Regional risks could also
include US-China trade and technology tensions.
Are geopolitical risks the new normal?

Document 383: Polish economic data supports our call for rate hikes in early 2027

Source PDF: all report pdf/Polish economic data supports our call for rate hikes in early 2027.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

Poland’s economy slowed in the third quarter, while core
inﬂation fell below 3% YoY. There is still little evidence of
second-round eﬀects. The MPC has nevertheless turned more
hawkish with staﬀ projecting high inﬂation next year,
supporting our base case of 50bp of rate hikes in early 2027

Rafal Benecki and Adam Antoniak
Poland’s economy slowed in the third quarter
GDP growth slowed in 3Q26, similar to
other economies in the region
According to the preliminary estimate, GDP growth slowed to 3.5%
YoY in 3Q26 from 4.1% YoY in 2Q26. Seasonally adjusted data point
to a slowdown in growth to 0.8% QoQ from 1.1% QoQ in the
previous quarter. We will learn the breakdown of GDP growth for
3Q26 on 30 November, but the available data suggests that growth
in industrial gross value added slowed, while construction activity
declined in annual terms. Continued solid growth in services was
not enough to oﬀset weaker performance in these areas.
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.

### Page 2

On the expenditure side, the main reason for slower growth last
quarter compared with 2Q26 was a deterioration in net exports, ie,
the foreign trade balance. Net exports added 0.5ppt to annual GDP
growth in 2Q26, while we estimate that the contribution turned
slightly negative in 3Q26. Private consumption growth probably
slowed further as well. With nominal wage growth normalising and
inﬂation rising, household incomes came under pressure. Higher

spending on fuel is limiting households’ ability to increase
consumption elsewhere. Geopolitical uncertainty also remains
elevated, resulting in a persistently high propensity to save.
Slower GDP growth, including weaker consumer demand, is an
important factor limiting inﬂationary pressure. This marks a
signiﬁcant diﬀerence from the previous energy shock in 2022, which
was accompanied by strong income growth and a consumption
boom. Under current conditions, it is more diﬃcult for companies to

raise prices and pass higher energy costs on to consumers. Price
competition in retail also remains intense, while Poland continues
to face strong competitive pressure from Chinese imports.

Source: GUS, ING
October CPI inﬂation conﬁrmed at 3.5%
YoY, with core inﬂation falling below 3%
YoY

### Page 3

According to the ﬁnal estimate, CPI inﬂation fell to 3.5% YoY in
October from 4.1% YoY in September. This was mainly due to a
decline in fuel prices (-9.7% MoM), related to cuts to excise duty and
VAT on petrol and diesel. Growth in food and non-alcoholic
beverage prices also remained low at 0.4% MoM, although we are
approaching the end of the period of annual deﬂation in this
category that began in June this year. The impact of the energy
crisis was, however, visible in housing energy prices, which rose by

1.7% MoM, mainly because of higher prices for solid fuels (7.4%
MoM, including an 11.2% MoM rise in coal) and liquid fuels (2.9%
MoM). Goods prices rose by 2.9% YoY and services prices by 4.9%
YoY, compared with 3.6% YoY and 5.3% YoY, respectively, in
September.
The biggest surprise in the October inﬂation breakdown, however,
was the decline in core inﬂation excluding food and energy prices.
According to our current estimates, it fell to 2.9% YoY from 3.1% YoY

in September, dropping below 3% YoY for the ﬁrst time since March.
Annual inﬂation in the recreation, sport and culture category fell
markedly, from 5.5% YoY to 4.0% YoY, mainly due to more
favourable tourism prices. Price pressure from transport services
also eased. Air fares fell by 10% MoM, although they remain 10%
higher than a year ago.
The higher energy commodity prices are not translating into
broader price pressures in the Polish economy, while the risk of

second-round eﬀects, ie, wage increases in response to inﬂation,
remains low.

in October

The National Bank of Poland’s (NBP) November inﬂation projection
points to a high inﬂation path in 2027, prompting a signiﬁcant

hawkish shift in the Monetary Policy Council (MPC) rhetoric. Recent
comments from Council members indicate a willingness to raise
interest rates in early 2027. This is consistent with our baseline
monetary policy scenario, which assumes a 50bp increase in the
cost of money in 1Q27.
The NBP remains more dovish than other central banks in the
region and core market policymakers, including the Fed and ECB,
and in our view could postpone rate hikes until early 2027. Data on

the October CPI composition conﬁrm that there is still no evidence
of second-round eﬀects. It's worth noting that monetary policy was
also more restrictive at the starting point than in other countries.
We believe the Council is waiting for information on the scale of
government measures to cushion electricity, gas and fuel prices at
the beginning of 2027.
The inﬂation scenario presented by NBP economic staﬀ is, however,
subject to considerable uncertainty. This applies to fuel prices on

global and Polish markets, as well as the scale of changes in
regulated energy prices, including electricity and gas bills, from the
beginning of 2027. Oil prices have moderated again, while the
government is preparing measures to freeze gas prices for
households.

Rates are back under upward pressure, reviving our tactical
bearish view. AI risks have become more idiosyncratic, but
upcoming catalysts could quickly reverse the move. France
remains under pressure, while Italy could see a rating upgrade

Michiel Tukker and Benjamin Schroeder
Tactically bearish on rates but AI event risk
ahead of us
Price action suggests global rates are happy to push higher again,
and we therefore return to our tactical bearish call. Risk sentiment is
improving, with AI worries turning more idiosyncratic, focused
mostly on NVIDIA’s leadership questions. Amid more stable risk
conditions, but without strong catalysts, the 2yr USD swap jumped
up by some 12bp on the day. Of course, one could argue oil ticked
higher and jobless numbers were robust, but nothing that would
warrant such a strong reaction by itself.
Buying the dip seems to be the recurring theme in equity markets,
but we warn of some key event risks ahead. Next week, we have

both Anthropic’s IPO pricing and NVIDIA announcing third-quarter

Europe is relatively more shielded from AI, but global risk sentiment

growth outlook improves materially. A global setback in AI would
push against this narrative, raising expectations for rate cuts after
the initial hiking cycle.
EGBs drift with the broader sentiment while

The mood in the European bond market has again darkened

somewhat. In part, this owed to the spillover from tech-related
equity jitters and unease around geopolitics after the latest
comments from US President Donald Trump, but the general
repricing higher of rates, which originated in the US, has done its
share as well.
France has lagged its peers with the 10y OAT/Bund back towards
150bp. There has been little news on the situation in France, but it is
worth noting that any hopes of ECB President Christine Lagarde

pulling France out of its political deadlock were dampened after the
ﬁrst polls this week showed she would get only 14% of the ﬁrst-
round votes, similar to other centrist candidates. This would still see
election frontrunner Marine Le Pen and left-wing rival Jean-Luc
Mélenchon advance to the second round, leaving one of the tail risk
scenarios on the table.

currently rates Italy BBB+ with a positive outlook. That outlook has

been in place since January, which would make a ﬁrst rating
upgrade back to single-A a possibility. Considering the more

Friday's events and market view
The European session will see a busy slate of ECB speakers,

据彭博此前报道， SpaceX 与特斯拉此前的⽬标是在明年上半年宣布交易，并⼒争在特朗普总统
任期结束前完成合并。
⻢斯克于 10 ⽉披露，两家公司的董事会已分别成⽴由独⽴董事组成的特别委员会，专⻔负责评
估合并事宜。⽬前，双⽅正在讨论的核⼼议题之⼀，是 SpaceX 在交易中需向特斯拉投资者发⾏
的股份数量。
值得关注的是，特斯拉定于 12 ⽉ 3 ⽇召开年度股东⼤会，这⼀时间节点与最快 12 ⽉宣布交易的时
间线⾼度重合。
两家公司此前已存在业务协同基础 —— 双⽅正联合在德克萨斯州建设⼀座名为 "Terafab" 的半导
体⼯⼚，并持续推进联合运营的前期布局。
监管与政治⻛险构成关键障碍
尽管谈判提速，交易⾯临的障碍不容忽视。据报道，知情⼈⼠指出，如何隔离特斯拉在中国的
业务，是双⽅必须解决的核⼼难题之⼀。

摩根⼤通分析师 Rajat Gupta 上⽉在研报中向客⼾指出， SpaceX 涉及美国政府及国防合同的国家
安全敏感性，与特斯拉在中国规模庞⼤且盈利丰厚的制造业务，构成 " 相互对⽴的关键动态 " ，将
对交易获得监管及股东批准形成重⼤挑战。
与此同时，此次合并也将成为美国中期选举后政治⽣态的重要试⾦⽯。⺠主党在此次中期选举
中重新掌控国会，并已承诺对⻢斯克的商业及政治活动展开调查，这为交易的推进增添了额外
的政治不确定性。
交易规模史⽆前例， AI 战略逻辑驱动合并
此次合并将在华尔街掀起轩然⼤波。合并后的实体将囊括电动汽⻋、储能电池、⽕箭及卫星业
务，合并体量按当前市值估算超过 3.4 万亿美元——SpaceX 市值逾 1.9 万亿美元，特斯拉市值约
1.5 万亿美元。
将上述资产整合于同⼀屋檐下的核⼼逻辑，在于双⽅共同的战略雄⼼：跨越太空、⽆⼈驾驶汽
⻋与⼈形机器⼈三⼤领域，全⾯布局⼈⼯智能。
SpaceX 正式名称为 Space Exploration Technologies Corp. ，总部位于得克萨斯州星际基地

（ Starbase ）。该公司于今年 6 ⽉在纽约完成具有⾥程碑意义的⾸次公开募股，此次上市也令⻢
斯克成为全球⾸位⾝家突破万亿美元的富豪。近年来， SpaceX 已从领先的⽕箭制造商和发射服
务商，转型为向 Anthropic PBC 等企业提供 AI 基础设施的关键供应商，同时也是全球顶级的太空
互联⽹服务提供商。
限免

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
3
OVERVIEW
Coming into the home strait for 2026,
the Australian consumer has again
got the staggers. Spending has held
up surprisingly well given the intense
headwinds that have buffeted household
budgets over the year. But the same cannot
be said for sentiment which has fallen off
badly in the face of more shocks.

Our October Red Book surveys the scene.
Psychologically, consumers has seen a
return to the ‘cost of living nightmare’
they struggled with through much of
2022, 2023 and 2024. Global forces
and domestic factors are again having
pervasive negative impacts on budgets.
The scale may not be as severe as the
previous episode – inflation is running in
the 3-4% range not the 7-8% and interest
rates have risen 1ppt not 4 – but the
feeling is similar. In part this because there

has was very little reprieve in between –
despite tax cuts and a partial unwind of the
interest rate tightening in 2025, household
incomes never got a chance to regain the
lost ground.
The inflation ‘relapse’, and the difficulty
the RBA and governments seem to be
having in managing the situation, is likely
feeding into fears about the outlook for the
wider economy as well.
Exactly what this means for spending is
not entirely clear. As discussed in our last

report, sentiment reads have been much
weaker than actual spending in recent
years. The apparent disconnect is likely
due to a mix of legacy effects from COVID,
and the specific challenges posed by high
inflation (including the lingering effects
of high prices even once inflation has
subsided).
On balance we expect the spending side
to slow materially, particularly as we
head into year end. Indeed, the seven
weeks between now and Christmas
could prove decisive for the consumer

outlook, beginning with another important
sentiment survey update and then the high
season for retail. How spending performs
outside of key sales windows will be most
telling.
Staggering into year end

1. Consumer spending: slowdown ahead
“... sentiment ... has fallen off badly in the face of

### Page 4

or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
4
The Westpac–Melbourne Institute
Consumer Sentiment Index declined 4.2%
over the three months to Oct, unwinding a
mini-rally through the middle of the year.
At 80.4 the average read over the Oct
survey implies that pessimists outnumber
optimists by nearly 20%, a deeply
pessimistic level by historical standards

that is amongst the forty worst reads since
the survey began in the 1973.
The survey detail shows concerns are still
primarily about the cost-of-living and high
interest rates than the more acute job and
insolvency problems that arise during a
recession. The RBA’s Sep rate hike, mid-
way through the survey week looks to have
badly rattled consumers.
The component detail shows a broad
pull-back but with bigger declines in
assessments of family finances and

buyer sentiment and milder declines in
assessments of the economic outlook.
Consumers remain intensely risk averse.
The Westpac Risk Aversion Index held near
historical highs in Sep, readings still only a
touch of the 52-year high of 60.8 recorded
in June 2023. Recall that the index is based
on responses to questions on the ‘wisest
place for savings’. Responses show ‘safe
options’ remain very heavily favoured.
The sentiment mix points to a significant

weakening in spending momentum is
weakening. CSI±, a modified indicator
that shows a better correlation with per
capita spending, points to a shift back to
significant outright contractions in per
capita spending. That said, spending
in 2026 has held up better than these
indicators have suggested so far.
Consumers are on high alert for interest
rate. The Westpac–Melbourne Institute
Mortgage Rate Expectations Index rose
5.5% to 179 over the three months to

Oct, just shy of May’s cycle peak of 181.
The RBA has raised interest rates 25bps
since then. The Nov survey update due Nov
10 will be an important update on how
expectations have evolved.
The ‘time to buy a major item’ index
dropped 4.3% over the last three months,
taking it back near the lows seen earlier
in the year and a full 40pts below the
long run avg. Durables spend has been
mixed over the first half of 2026, posting a
surprisingly solid gain in Q2 but with most

of this centred on new vehicle spend where
‘extenuating circumstances’ were at play.
Homebuyer sentiment has been
understandably unsettled in recent months
given heightened uncertainty around
interest rate and tax policy changes amid
evidence of a broadening downturn in
established markets.
The ‘time to buy a dwelling’ index actually
lifted 3.6% over the three months to Oct
but is still at firmly pessimistic levels. Both
monthly reads and sub-group detail have

been very unsettled.
The broadening and deepening downturn
in Australia’s housing markets is seeing
an equally rapid shift in consumer house
price expectations although these have not
turned as negative as they did during the
2022-23 correction.
The Westpac–Melbourne Institute
Consumer House Price Expectations Index
declined a further 2.4% over the 3mths to
Oct, following a steep 25% drop over the
previous 3mths. At 115.6, the Oct index
read is miles below the 15yr high of 174 at

the start of the year and below the long run
avg of 130. That said, the read is still above
100, meaning more consumers expect
prices to rise than fall.
The labour market remains an island of
relative calm for consumers. The Westpac–
Melbourne Institute Unemployment
Expectations Index rose 9.3% over the
three months to Oct, reversing most of
the 12.1% improvement over the previous
three months (recall that higher reads mean
more consumers expect unemployment to

rise over the year ahead). At 142.2, the Oct
index read is now decisively above the long
run avg of 129 again but  still well below
the levels that would sound more worrying
alarms.
As noted, the Nov survey, currently in the
field and due to be released on Nov 10,
will provide critical updates on many of
these themes.
Quick run-down

### Page 5

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
5
THE CONSUMER MOOD
• Consumers remain stuck in a cost-of-
living nightmare that seems to have
not end in sight. Sentiment remains
deeply pessimistic, weighed down by
continued uncertainty about the path
for inflation and interest rates, the
latter providing a clear negative ‘jolt’
midway through the Oct survey week.

• The Westpac Melbourne Institute
Index of Consumer Sentiment declined
4.2% over the three months to Oct,
unwinding a mini-rally through the
middle of the year. At 80.4 the average
read over the Oct survey implies that
pessimists outnumber optimists by
nearly 20%, a deeply pessimistic level
by historical standards. It is amongst
the forty worst reads since the survey
began in the 1973. We have had
two others in this unfortunate group
already this year (in Apr and Jun) with

another eight in 2022–23. Altogether
this marks the worst period of recurring
extremely weak sentiment since the
disastrous recession in the early 1990s.
• The survey detail shows consumer
concerns are still primarily about the
cost-of-living and high interest rates
than the more acute job and insolvency
problems that arise during a recession.
• Average weekly pump prices pushed
back over $2.30/litre nationally in early
Oct, rising to over $2.35/litre since. This

is near the peaks seen back in April and
up nearly 25% since the start of the
year. Meanwhile the RBA interest rate
rise in late Sep took the cash rate to
4.6%, the highest since 2011, with the
standard variable mortgage interest
rate pushing above 9% for the first time
since 2008.
• The RBA’s Sep move badly rattled
consumers. Sentiment amongst the
60% of the sample surveyed prior came
in at 86.9, up slightly from the Sep
read. The sentiment read across the

40% surveyed after the RBA decision
came in at just 67.2, an alarmingly weak
read that, for complete surveys, has
only been registered during the depths
of the early 1990s recession. The nearly
20% drop between pre and post RBA
samples is also the largest negative
swing we have seen since we started
tracking daily responses in 2019.
• The RBA has delivered another hike as
we go to press, meaning Nov is likely
to see yet another interest rate hike

to sentiment (the survey result will be
released on Nov 10).

3. Consumer sentiment: finances, economic conditions

### Page 6

or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
6
THE CONSUMER MOOD
• The component detail shows a broad
pull-back but with bigger declines in
assessments of family finances and
buyer sentiment. The ‘finances vs a
year ago’ sub-index dropped 6% over
the 3mths to Oct  to just 66.9, on a par
with April’s extreme low. The pressure
is not expected to ease up any time

soon, the ‘finances, next 12 months’
sub-index also down 8.3% to 88.4, in
the bottom 10% of reads for this sub-
index since the survey began.
• Consumer views on the economy
recorded milder declines, the ‘economy,
next 12 months’ sub-index unchanged
on Jul, holding at 78.3 and the
‘economy, next 5 years’ sub-index down
1.8% to 85.5. However, the latter is now
back near the lows seen in Aug 2020.
This was a dark time during the COVID
crisis when Melbourne had entered

its second lockdown and just prior to
the Federal government significantly
expanding its JobKeeper supports. The
readings suggest there are shades of
COVID now with a similar sense of
helplessness – that Australia is unable
to avoid a global inflation problem and
that there is relatively little authorities
can do to help the situation that does
not add to pressures on consumers.
• The cost of living and interest rate
pressures are both broad and intense.

Pessimists outnumber optimists in 102
of the 106 sub-groups we track, and by
over 20% in nearly two thirds of these.
This speaks to the pervasive influence
of higher fuel costs in particular.
• That said, sentiment is becoming more
dispersed. It is possible to compute
sentiment index scores for each
individual respondent. Chart 4 shows
how the spread across these scores
has evolved since 1995. The average
standard deviation over the full history
is 46pts. It was about 4pts lower over

the decade prior to COVID but has risen
steadily since to over 52pts in Oct, the
average spread over the last 12mths
marking a record high. Chart 5 shows
the full distribution of individual scores
in the last 3mths compared to history.
• We noted in our last report that the
widening spread has been accompanied
by a notable divergence in sentiment
across major and minor party voters.
There are likely multiple underlying
reasons for the shift which the voter

group split may be picking up indirectly
rather than directly due to the way these
skew across different demographics.

### Page 7

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
7
Spending: squeeze ahead
• Our CSI± composite combines sub-
indexes tracking views on ‘family
finances’ and ‘time to buy a major
item’ with the Westpac Consumer Risk
Aversion Index and usually provides a
good guide to trends in spending over
the next 3–6mths.
• However, as discussed in detail in our
last report the composite has been of

more limited use in recent years due
to both the volatile spending around
COVID lock-downs and the nature of
the post-COVID cost-of-living shock
to sentiment. Analysis shows adding
the WestpacMelbourne Institute Index
of Unemployment Expectations (p15)
to the composite goes some way to
redressing these issues. Accordingly,
our CSI± indicator will now include this
additional component going forward.
As Chart 6 highlights, the composite
should still mainly be viewed as a

directional guide to where per capita
spending growth momentum is heading
rather than a point estimate.
•  Unsurprisingly, the CSI± indicator fell
steeply over the 3mths to Oct, dropping
from 75.6 to 67.6, putting the measure
over 26pts below its long run avg of
93.8.
• The move reflected the more marked
falls in assessments of finances
and ‘time to buy’ with a significant
deterioration in unemployment
expectations as well. Risk aversion
remained unchanged at very high levels.

• Chart 6 shows the current read is
consistent with a large contraction in
real per capita consumer spending,
even allowing for the wedge that has
opened up between observed spending
growth and the CSI± indicator over the
last few years.
• The Q2 national accounts showed some
slowing in consumer spending although
it was more resilient than feared to the
combined effect of higher fuel prices
and interest rates while incomes posted
a robust rise. More generally a range

of shocks and shifts made for a messy
quarter (see here for more).
• Real consumption rose 0.4%qtr taking
annual growth to 1.8%yr, down from
a peak of 2.7%yr in Q3 last year. The
detail showed declines in fuel spend
and transport services (including air
travel) were more than offset by a 10%
surge in vehicle sales, led by a big rise
in EVs and hybrids.

7. Annual national accounts: benchmark revisions
SENTIMENT INDICATORS

### Page 8

or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
8
• Household disposable incomes rose
0.6%qtr, lifting annual growth to
2.3%yr, supported by a continued
robust rise in labour income (more due
to average compensation than head
count in the latest quarter). This in
turn meant the household savings rate
ticked up slightly from 6.4% to 6.5%.
• New annual benchmarks released by

the ABS since the Q2 accounts were
published showed relatively minor
revisions (Chart 7). For the full financial
year, annual growth in disposable
income was nudged 0.2pts lower to
2.1%yr with real spending shaved back
from 1.8%yr to 1.7%yr and the savings
rate lowered from 6.5% to 6.1%. These
changes will be rolled into the quarterly
estimates when the Q3 national
accounts are released on Dec 2.
• Partial data suggests Q3 has seen a lift
in nominal spend although most of this

is a price effect. The ABS household
spending indicator, which covers around
two thirds of the total consumption
basket, showed a 2.1%qtr rise in nominal
terms in Q3 up from 1.3%qtr in Q2.
However, all of that was due to stronger
price growth with volumes posting a
0.7% rise in line with Q2 (see here).
• Our Westpac-DataX Card Tracker Index
foreshadowed the spending indicator
result and suggests quarterly growth
momentum is still tracking at around 2%

in mid-Oct in nominal terms. That said,
quarterly growth rate is likely slowing in
real terms given our estimates that Oct
saw a further step-up in price growth.
The latest monthly pace of gains also
looks to be about flat in nominal terms
(see Chart 8).
• Private sector business surveys point
to weak conditions for retail (consistent
with under-performing discretionary
goods segments) and stalling activity
for consumer services. Near term
expectations are at similar levels for

both but with year-weak expectations
flat rather than negative for retail.
• The ABS will publish full spending
estimates with the Q3 national accounts
on Dec 2. Based on partials we expect
these to show a solid +0.8%qtr gain,
lifting annual growth to 2.1%yr, an
upgrade on the +0.4%qtr, 1.5%yr
forecast in or last report. However, that’s
expected to be the high water mark for
growth with forecast to slow abruptly
into year end and early 2027, growth

tracking back to 1.4%yr by Q3 2027.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
9
Interest rates: hike fears realised
SENTIMENT INDICATORS
• Consumers have been on high alert for
interest rate rises for some time. Those
fears materialised in late Sep and again
in early Nov. The Oct survey was in the
field during the week of the Sep move.
Not surprisingly, the Westpac–Melbourne
Institute Mortgage Rate Expectations

Index, which tracks consumer interest
rate expectations, rose 5.5% to 179, just
shy of May’s cycle peak of 181.
• Amongst those surveyed after the RBA
decision, just over 80%, an overwhelming
majority, expect mortgage rates to
increase further over the next 12mths.
This compares to 64% in Sep. Across
the mortgage belt, the share is closer to
90%, with over 40% of this sub-group
expecting rates to rise by more than a
percentage point over the next twelve

months.
• While the Sep and Nov interest rate
increases were widely expected, the
scale of the rise over the last 12mths,
a full 1.25%, has come as a surprise.
This time last year, consumers were in
two minds, 39% of those with a view
expecting interest rates to rise and 37%
expecting them to fall. Only 20% of
consumers expected mortgage rates to
rise by over 1ppt.
• As at Oct, just over 40% of consumers
with a view expected mortgage interest
rates to rise by more than 1ppt. While

some hawkishness is clearly warranted,
this is extreme – it would take the
standard variable mortgage rate well
above 10%, to levels not seen since the
mid-1990s, thirty years ago.
• Overall, Oct responses suggest
consumers were broadly expecting
another three 25bp rate hikes from
the RBA. The Nov survey, currently in
the field, will capture how expectations
have shifted after the RBA’s more recent
move. While this may have amplified
rate rise fears, the split vote and some

indications from the Governor that there
may be a pause to assess things in Dec
could work the other way (see here for
more).
• The near term inflation outlook
clearly remains challenging. However,
Westpac expects the RBA to leave
rates on hold through the remainder
of 2026. And while there are risks
of a further tightening in early 2027
we think rates will more likely be
maintained at current levels with an
eventual gradual relaxation in policy
beginning in the second half of 2027.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
10
Inflation: still too hot but wages flat
SENTIMENT INDICATORS
•  Inflation has kicked up again over
the last 3mths, led by a resurgence
in global energy prices. Near term
consumer inflation expectations have
risen sharply again as well although
consumer wage expectations have not
moved, if anything moving marginally

lower in the latest month.
• The Sep CPI report showed headline up
1.3%qtr, the annual pace holding at the
Jun qtr pace of 3.9%yr. The key ‘trimmed
mean’ measure lifted 1.0%qtr, the
annual pace ticking up from 3.6%yr to
3.7%yr. The detail revealed underlying
inflationary pressures continuing even
while a moderation in housing inflation
(dwellings in particular) is unfolding
slightly faster than anticipated. Near-
term risks for inflation are to the upside

but more balanced further out (see here)
• Consumer inflation expectations
have moved higher again. After
peaking at 5.9% in Apr, the Melbourne
Institute’s ‘symmetric mean’ measure
of consumers’ year-ahead inflation
expectations eased back to 4.7% in Jul,
ticking up slightly to 4.9% in Aug and
Sep but jumped again back to 5.3%
in Jul. Expectations are likely being
skewed by the jump in fuel prices.
• Scaling according to historical inflation
outcomes suggests current expectations

are consistent with inflation of around
4%yr. It is difficult to look through the
effects of fuel price volatility, which
amplified expectations in Mar and Apr
but dampened them again when the
temporary halving in fuel excise tax
came into effect through Apr-May.
• From the RBA’s perspective, it will be
important for inflation expectations
to track back towards historical levels
in a timely manner as a sign that
expectations more generally remain

firmly anchored at low inflation rates.
• Certainly there are no signs that the
latest lift in inflation is becoming
embedded into expectations for wages.
Consumer assessments of actual wages
lifted a little when the new Award
decision came into effect but have
settled back to 1.4%, a touch above the
Jun qtr avg of 1.2% but below the long
run avg of 1.8%. Expectations have
tracked a slightly softer path, dipping
to 1.2% in Oct, down from a Jun qtr avg

of 1.3% and long run avg of 1.3%. At
face value, expectations are consistent
with a significant deterioration in real
wages.

Durables: soft spend, dire sentiment
SENTIMENT INDICATORS
• Durables spend has been mixed 
over the first half of 2026, posting 
a  surprisingly solid gain in Q2 but 
with most of this centred on new 
vehicle spend where ‘extenuating 
circumstances’ were at play. Even 
excluding this, the flat underlying trend 
is not nearly as weak as the dire reads 
on the ‘time to buy a major item’ sub-
index would suggest. The last 3mths 
has seen the sub-index drop back 
again, a 4.3% decline taking it to 83, 
near the lows seen earlier in the year 
and a full 40pts below the long-run avg 
of 123. 
• As discussed regularly over the last four 
years, the extreme weakness appears 
to relate to the collapse in purchasing 
power since 2022. While prices have 
stabilised somewhat, their still high 
level is casting a dark shadow over 
buyer sentiment. 
• As Chart 18 shows, durable goods prices 
have been more stable since 2024 but 
look to have posted a bigger rise in the 
Sep quarter, our estimates based on 
the CPI detail pointing to annual price 
growth lifting back above 2.1%yr.

• There are a range factors at play here 
for prices. Construction costs have fed 
into the renovation segment of our 
measure. Some major durable items also 
have higher transport costs components. 
However, a notable medium term trend 
has been the absence of trend price 
declines for audio-visual and computing 
equipment, which make up a large part 
of the ‘small durables’ segment. 
• As chart 15 shows, growth in real 
per capita spend on the ‘major item’ 
segments centred on a 10%qtr jump in 
vehicles in Q2, led by strong take-up 
of EVs that was partly in response to 
the sharp move higher in fuel prices 
this year. Excluding vehicles, per capita 
spend was up just 0.3%qtr, 0.8%yr. 
• Partial data suggests Q3 is softer with 
the industry data pointing to a much 
slower 1%qtr gain for vehicle sales 
and the ABS Household Spending 
Indicator detail showing flat volumes for 
furnishings and household equipment. 
Our card data also suggests there 
has been a further shift to outright 
weakness in discretionary goods in the 
Oct month.

Dwellings: pessimistic and uncertain
SENTIMENT INDICATORS
•  Housing-related sentiment has been 
understandably unsettled in recent 
months, reflecting the heightened 
uncertainty around interest rate and 
tax policy changes amid evidence of a 
broadening downturn in established 
markets. 
• Assessments of ‘time to buy’ – 
generally seen as being from the 
perspective of owner occupiers rather 
than investors – have been choppy 
month to month but still in firmly 
pessimistic territory, albeit off the 
extreme lows seen in previous years. 
• The ‘time to buy a dwelling’ index 
actually lifted 3.6% over the 3mths to 
Oct sharply in May (–16%) but tracked 
a volatile monthly path, up 12% at one 
stage but unwinding most of that as 
inflation and rate rise fears returned. 
• At 88.4, the Oct index read is still far 
from positive, over 30pts below the 
long run avg of 119.

• The sub-group detail is also quite 
unsettled. The Oct month improvement 
was more pronounced for outright 
homeowners, retirees, 25–34-year-olds 
and those earning over $80k a year. 
But on a quarterly basis the biggest 
improvement has been amongst those 
aged 50+ and earning over $100k. 
• Interestingly, existing owners of 
residential property investments (25% 
of all respondents) continue to be 
outright positive on ‘time to buy’.
•  By state, buyer sentiment is still a little 
less downbeat in NSW and Vic (both 
state indexes at 93), where dwelling 
prices have seen bigger moves lower, 
but more pessimistic in Qld (85) and 
deeply pessimistic in WA (71) and SA 
(70). 
• The overarching question is whether 
improving affordability – reflecting a 
mix of lower prices and higher interest 
rates – will be sufficient to offset 
unease about the pace of price declines.

13
House prices: uncertain vs downbeat
SENTIMENT INDICATORS
• The broadening and deepening 
downturn in Australia’s housing 
markets is seeing an equally rapid shift 
in consumer house price expectations 
although these have not turned as 
negative as they did during the 2022-23 
correction. 
• The Westpac Melbourne Institute House 
Price Expectations Index declined a 
further 2.4% over the 3mths to Oct, 
following a steep 25% drop over the 
previous 3mths. At 115.6, the Oct index 
read is miles below the 15yr high of 
174 at the start of the year and below 
the long run avg of 130. 
• That said, the read is still above 100, 
meaning more consumers expect prices 
to rise than fall. This compares to the 
low of 91 during the 2022-23 correction 
when the consensus view was for 
continued declines. The Nov survey 
update will be an important gauge 
of whether a negative consensus has 
again emerged about the house price 
outlook. If it does, price momentum is 
likely to remain weak as buyers will be 
more inclined to hold off and sellers 
more willing to accept low prices now.

• The detailed responses in Oct were a 
picture of uncertainty, 46% of those 
surveyed expecting prices to rise, 18% 
expecting no change, 31% expecting 
prices to decline and 5% reporting 
“don’t know”. This was a similar mix to 
Jul.  
• That said, there is little doubt that 
the downturn is deepening. The 
Cotality home value index – covering 
all of Australia – fell 1.2% in Oct, 
annual growth dropping into negative 
(–2.8%yr) for the first time and prices 
down 6.7% from their peak in Mar (see 
here for a full run down). 
• Actual price growth has been broadly 
comparable to consumer house price 
expectations overall (see Chart 18), 
although the latest monthly result 
suggests the latter is set to move 
materially lower. By state, expectations 
have overshot outcomes in NSW and 
Vic, been very closely aligned in Qld but 
undershot to date in WA. 
• Notably, the sub-group detail shows 
price expectations have outright 
pessimistic (sub-100) for older age 
groups.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
14
Risk aversion: bracing
SENTIMENT INDICATORS
•  Consumers remain intensely risk 
averse. The Westpac Consumer Risk 
Aversion Index remained near historical 
extreme highs in Sep, dipping slightly 
from 60.1 to 57.5, but unchanged in 
seasonally adjusted terms (as shown 
on Chart 20). Readings are just a touch 
of the 52-year high of 60.8 recorded in 
June 2023.  
•  Recall that the index is based on 
responses to questions on the ‘wisest 
place for savings’. These ask consumers 
to nominate from a range of options, the 
risk index being the difference between 
the share nominating ‘safe’ options such 
as ‘deposits’ and ‘repay debt’ and the 
share nominating ‘risky’ options such as 
‘shares’ and ‘real estate’. 
• ‘Safe options’ remain very heavily 
favoured: 33% of consumers 
nominating ‘bank deposits’; 24% 
nominating ‘pay down debt’ and 7% 
nominating ‘superannuation’. The share 
nominating ‘real estate’ hold near 
historic lows at just 4.7%.

•  As noted previously, the mix of high 
inflation, interest rate rises, tax policy 
changes, a housing downturn, and 
numerous other factors – including 
conflict in the Middle East, tariffs, 
and a range of disruptions from AI 
– is creating an extremely uncertain 
environment for consumers when it 
comes to financial decisions. 
• This ‘safety first’ mode is typically 
associated with more elevated savings 
rates, increased voluntary debt 
repayments and an aversion to new 
borrowing that drives debt to income 
ratios lower over time. Notably, mix 
of responses on ‘repay debt’ suggests 
the focus is primarily on non mortgage 
rather than mortgage debt.
• The survey detail showed a fair bit 
of variation across sub-groups, with 
material declines in Vic and SA, 35-44 
year olds, retirees and males but rises 
in NSW and amongst 45-54 year olds. 
Note that all of these reads were taken 
from the Sep survey and so pre-date 
the RBA’s rate rise in late Sep.

15
Job security: a wind shift?
SENTIMENT INDICATORS
• For Australian consumers, the labour 
market remains an island of relative 
calm amid the swirling mix of cost-of-
living stresses, rate rise and housing 
downturns. Job-loss fears have moved 
around, most recently rising again over 
the last three months, but the moves 
have been mild compared to wider 
sentiment swings and the overall level 
of concern still relatively mild.  
• The Westpac-Melbourne Institute 
Index of Unemployment Expectations 
rose 9.3% over the three months to 
Oct, reversing most of the 12.1% 
improvement over the previous three 
months (recall that higher reads mean 
more consumers expect unemployment 
to rise over the year ahead). At 142.2, 
the Oct index read is now decisively 
above the long run avg of 129 again but  
still well below the levels that would 
sound more worrying alarms.
• The latest ABS labour force figures 
show employment gains have been 
lumpy month to month but have 
averaged +34k/mth through May-Aug, a 
tick up from the +22k/mth average over 
the six months prior.

• Annual employment growth has 
been slower, holding within the 1.0-
1.5%yr range over the past year or 
so. With the working age population 
expanding 1.7%yr, the mix has seen 
the unemployment rate drift higher, 
reaching 4.7% in Aug vs 4.4% in 
May (see here for more). While there 
is evidence of a little more slack 
emerging in labour markets this 
appears to be mainly coming from a 
combination of increased supply and 
more moderate growth in hiring rather 
than retrenchments. Unfortunately 
changes to the ABS survey mean official 
estimates of both retrenchments and 
expected retrenchment (as shown in 
Chart 26) are unavailable.    
• Based on historical outcomes and 
adjusting for biases, the latest index 
read is roughly consistent with a 0.4-
0.6ppt rise in the unemployment rate, 
which would take it into the 5.2-5.3% 
range by this time next year.
• The sub-group detail shows real 
estate and construction workers led 
the deterioration in Oct, suggesting it 
is linked to interest rate and housing 
market developments.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
16
Queensland
•   The last time we profiled consumer
sentiment in Qld, a year ago, it was
seeing a marked divergence with
the rest of Aus with Qlders much
more pessimistic. Some of that
shift appeared to be linked to the
withdrawal of what were more material
cost-of-living supports provided by
the Qld state government, and an

associated sharper rise in headline
inflation. That in turn suggested
the negative wedge on sentiment
could close up a little. And for a time
this was the case: Qld more-or-less
re-converged sentiment-wise with
the rest of Aus around the start of
2026. However, since then, sentiment
amongst the state’s consumers has
deteriorated more sharply again. At just
76.3 in Oct, the ‘Sunshine state’ is the
gloomiest of the big 3 eastern states
and plumbing 2022-23 lows.

• Component-wise, the 6pt gap with the
rest of Aus in recent months has mainly
centred on differing assessments of the
economic outlook where there has been
a 7-8pt wedge. However, assessments
of finances deteriorated particularly
sharply in the Oct month suggesting the
housing downturn, which came on later
in Qld, may be a factor now as well.
• Qld’ers are also more pessimistic
on time to buy questions (especially
dwellings) but interestingly are less

troubled about unemployment.
• The latter may speak to a different
take on the wider economic and
inflation challenges: as a state, Qld
has tended to see stronger demand
growth (including an expected lift in
infrastructure and Olympics-related
build) that it putting more pressure
on capacity, particularly across its
labour market. Qld’s unemployment
rate is sitting at 4.5% a touch below
the national rate which is unusual
for a state that has tended to see

unemployment track slightly above the
national rate for most of its history (not
because of labour demand issues but
because the strength and composition
of its population growth has tended to
see more ample supply. This excessive
fear factor may be a view in Qld that
Australia’s capacity and inflation
problems may be more substantive and
enduring.
• Looking ahead, housing may be the
next area to weigh more heavily on
sentiment with prices in the state now

showing a very abrupt slowing (p13).

科技財報仍以AI展望最樂觀
分析師／張乘維、謝孟璇、張偉漢
研究範疇／科技
科技產業投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/10/30
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《AI晶片與相關硬體獲利持續創新高》
根據機構LSEG統計，受到AI晶片、記憶體、硬碟等
科技公司獲利優於預期，機構上調科技產業4Q26、
1Q27獲利年增率到56.2%、54.6%，本行樂觀看待
科技產業發展後勢。10月份重要科技硬體晶圓代工
龍頭台積電3Q26法說表示，受益於強勁AI需求與2
奈米開始貢獻營收，公司3Q26營收與獲利均優於市
場預期。3Q26高效運算與智慧手機營收比重分別達
68%、21%。高效運算營收年增高達53%、智慧手
機年減10%。AI代理與開源模型普及帶動先進製程

用量， 台 積 電 預 估 4Q26 美 元 營 收 將 年 增 62% ，
2026年美元營收預估達44%。由於AI晶片成長高於
60%，預估台積電2027年營收與獲利仍將續創新高。
市場投資展望
資產 4Q26看法 投資展望
科技 正向 台積電、英特爾、類比晶片財報顯示資料中心需求仍遠優於消費電子 終端，企業對於AI相關軟體支出持續增溫
• 雲端巨頭資本支出持平，伺服器
仍優於消費終端 Pg2
• 類比晶片終端需求上升，工業、
資料中心展望佳 Pg3
• 券商 CIO 調查顯示軟體支出預期
持續增溫 Pg4
• IT 支出首選AI/ML/自動化相關 Pg5

2
重點摘要
• 雲端巨頭資本支出持平，伺
服器仍優於消費終端
《雲端巨頭資本支出持平，伺服器仍優於消費終端》
3Q26財報顯示，谷哥、亞馬遜、微軟與Meta在
2026年資本支出目標合計達7,300億美元，較上季
略減25億美元(-0.3%)，減少主要是微軟外部第三方
算力產能延遲導致資本支出目標下修50億美元，與
Meta上調25億美元相互抵消。雖然雲端巨頭支出
持平但資料中心晶片需求仍強勁。英特爾3Q26財報
顯示，受益於資料中心與AI事業帶動營收達165億
美元、年增21%。其中資料中心與人工智慧相關營
收73億美元續創歷史新高、年增78%，2027年相關
晶片仍將供不應求。反觀個人電腦營收81億美元、
年減5%。英特爾預估2026年個人電腦出貨量將年

減12%、2027年受到記憶體與儲存供給影響預估將
續減10%。
受到上半年消費者預期終端售價上漲提前換機，
3Q26個人電腦與智慧手機出貨均呈現大幅下滑。根
據IDC統計，3Q26全球個人電腦出貨量達6,270萬
台，較2025年同期衰退20%。同樣受到記憶體漲價
與供應短缺衝擊的智慧手機3Q26出貨量2.6億台，
較2025年同期衰退22%。由於未來記憶體與儲存價
格小幅調漲以及供給受限下，消費終端價格仍將維
持高價位。因此，IDC預估個人電腦與智慧手機
2027年將高機率維持衰退，預估要2028年出貨量
才有望復甦。
資料來源：公司，2026/10/30，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/30

吸納量
重點摘要
3
《類比晶片終端需求上升，工業、資料中心占比較
高供應商營收更具成長性》
德州儀器(TXN)3Q26營收60.6億美元、EPS 2.62美
元雙雙優於預期，4Q26財測營收58-63億美元，中
位數較市場普遍預期高出6%，反映資料中心市場
需求持續成長，使營收預測優於往年季節性回落。
以3Q26終端市場來看，工業營收年增超過40%、
季增約10%，成長來自航太國防、能源基礎設施等
領域；車用營收年增約15%、季增約10%，受益於
客戶庫存水準降低及電動車占比提高；資料中心營
收則年增約95%、季增20%，為成長最快的終端市
場。在營運方面，產能利用率提高以及2027年潛在
的價格調漲可能進一步擴大獲利成長空間，儘管資

本支出可能高於2026年的20~30億美元，但顯示終
端需求正擴大成長。
恩智浦(NXPI)3Q26營收37.9億美元、EPS 4.20美元
優於預期，但4Q26財測營收中位數38.5億美元略
低於市場預期。以3Q26終端市場來看，車用營收
20.7億美元、年增13%，成長主要來自軟體定義汽
車(SDV)產品貢獻；工業與物聯網營收8.02億美元、
年增39%，主要由資料中心、工廠自動化和醫療保
健等產業帶動；通訊基礎設施營收5.03億美元、年
增54%，得益於資料中心業務成長。恩智浦財報結
果顯示各終端市場需求持續上升，惟成長速度較慢
的車用營收占比超過五成，未來成長速度可能不及
工業和資料中心營收占比更高的同業。
• 德州儀器4Q26 財 測 營 收 較
市場預期高出6%，反映資料
中心市場需求大幅成長。
• 德儀產能利用率提高及2027
年潛在的價格調漲可能進一
步擴大獲利成長空間。
• 恩智浦3Q26營收、EPS優於
市場預期，各終端市場營收
皆呈年增。
• 恩智浦4Q26財測低 於預期，

反映其成長速度較慢的車用
營收占比超過五成。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/28，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/30

重點摘要
零售客流量表
• 券商 CIO 調查顯示 IT 採購預
算與軟體支出預期持續增溫。
• AI/ML 與網安軟體優先性持續
領先，但 ERP、BI、CRM 等
應用軟體優先性也見改善。
4
《券商 CIO 調查顯示軟體支出預期持續增溫》
券商 CIO 調查指出 2027 年的企業 IT 採購預算仍維
持成長。CIO 現在預計 2026 年的 IT 採購預算將年
增 4.0%，自 2Q26 走高 20bps，連續 3 次增溫。
對於 2027 年 IT 採購預算的首次估計，CIO 預期年
增 4.0%，雖僅與更新後的 2026 年預期相符，但實
則是近 4 年來的開年新高。在這之中，軟體預算成
長優於領先硬體、網通與服務，預期年增 4.4%，且

自 2026 年加速 10bps。2027 年軟體支出的預期加
速可能進一步說明短期內 AI 並未帶來全面性衝擊。
資料來源：摩根史丹利，2026/10/12，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/20
人工智慧與機器學習 (AI/ML) 的支出優先性仍穩居
CIO 調查的第一名，佔比雖較前幾期調研回落，但
這應該是因為 CIO 對於開始增加其他項目的優先性。
AI 相關專案的預算仍最不可能被刪除，且淨占比
(即最不可能被刪除 – 最可能被刪除的比例)，3Q26
較 2Q26 大幅走揚 13ppts 至 17%。網路安全的支
出優先性則連 4 季走揚，且最不可能被刪除的淨占
比也走揚 1ppt 至 16%。持續反映出 AI 帶動的攻擊
面擴大、攻擊手法更加多元，與攻擊成本大幅走低，
導致企業在 AI 顯著產生正面效益前，優先選擇防範
未然的心態。而隨著 AI 的能力仍在快速發展，如近
期 Anthropic 研究即指出 AI 已可自我迭代，且小
型模型未來可能具備在物聯網裝置上自我複製的能

力，本行預期網安的需求可望仍持續強健。數位轉
型雖仍處第 3，但在本次券商調查中大幅地低於過
硬體 軟體 通訊設備 IT服務
■3Q25 ■4Q25 ■1Q26 ■2Q26 ■3Q26
2026 外部 IT 支出期望

更強的信心。

去數季，且相較過往也是較低水位。相反地，本次

這或許與 CIO 們對於如何達成企業內 AI 能力的偏

自建。這在一定程度上反映出企業軟體在移轉成本、

人可更加關注各應用軟體在此領域推進的表現。

生産
8月の鉱工業生産（関西）は前月比－2.6%と2ヵ月連続でマイナスとなり、足踏み状態となっている。主要業種
の動きをみると、化学が増加したが、電子部品・デバイス、電気機械、汎用・生産用・業務用機械が減少した。
先行きは、持ち直しを見込むが、米・イランの対立長期化、中国経済の減速などから、下振れるリスクがある。

日銀短観9月調査の業況判断DI（「良い」－「悪い」）は、全産業で＋21ptと6月調査（＋17ｐｔ）から改善。製
造業、非製造業ともに改善した。業種別に見ると、製造業では化学、非鉄金属、はん用・生産用・業務用機
械などが、非製造業は建設、物品賃貸、対個人サービスなどが改善している。先行き（12月）については、

Document 515: 不動産市場で存在感を増す私募ファンド ～多様な運用戦略とREITとの補完関係～

Source PDF: all report pdf/不動産市場で存在感を増す私募ファンド ～多様な運用戦略とREITとの補完関係～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Flash Insights

2026 年 11 月 6 日

1/4 
不動産市場で存在感を増す私募ファンド 
～多様な運用戦略と REIT との補完関係～ 
 資産運用調査部 シニア研究員  河谷 善夫

1．大きく残高を増やしてきた私募ファンド 
資料 1 は、我が国の不動産証券化の主体別保有資産額の推移と、2015 年度から 2025 年度までの
年平均増加率（CAGR）を示したものである。

資料 1 不動産証券化の主体別保有資産額の推移（左）と年平均増加率（CAGR）（右） 
<保有資産額の推移>    <年平均増加率（CAGR）>

（注 1）J-REIT、私募リート、不動産特定共同事業は各年度末、私募ファンドは各年度の 12 月末時点。年平均増加率は、それぞ
れの系列の 2015 年度と 2025 年度の比較値から算出。なお、資産額は、保有する不動産・信託受益権の残高を示し、年間の
取得額や投資家の出資額ではない。 
（注 2）不動産特定共同事業とは、不動産特定共同事業法に基づき、国土交通大臣や都道府県知事などの許可・登録を受けて
行われるもの。複数の投資家から出資金を募り、不動産を売買・賃貸・運用して得られた利益を分配する事業。不動産クラウド
ファンディングの多くが活用し、少額からの投資が可能となっている。 
（注 3）不動産私募ファンドには、広義には私募リートも含まれる。私募リートは、投信法に基づく非上場の投資法人である。運用
期間を定めず、一定の条件の下で投資口の払戻しに応じる（オープンエンド）。一方、それ以外の私募ファンドには、合同会社
や特定目的会社等を用い、運用期間を定め、投資家の請求による期中の払戻しを行わないものが多い。本稿では、私募リート
を除く不動産私募ファンドを「私募ファンド」と呼ぶ。なお、私募リート以外の私募ファンドにも、無期限・オープンエンド型の商品
がある。 
（出所）国土交通省「不動産証券化の実態調査」及びARES J-REIT Databook（2026 年）を基に第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作
成

2015 年度時点の比較では、J-REITの保有資産額が最も大きかったが、2020 年代に入り、私募フ
ァンド、私募リートの保有資産額は、J-REITを上回るペースで増加している。 
2025 年度時点でみると、私募ファンドの保有資産額は約39.5 兆円（2025 年 12 月末）と、J-

### Page 2

2/4 
REITの約24.5 兆円（2026 年3 月末）を約 15 兆円上回っている。この間の年平均増加率で最も大
きいのは、私募リートだが、私募ファンドも年11％を超える伸びを示した。 
もっとも、保有資産には借入金を活用して取得したものも含まれるため、その残高増加を、投
資家からの新規出資額の増加と同一視することはできない。また、残高の増加だけで運用成績を
判断することもできない。一方、不動産の保有・運用を担う主体として、私募ファンドの存在感
が増していることは確認できる。近年の拡大ペースの違いを考えるうえでは、資金調達経路の違
いも重要である。J-REITでは、投資口価格の低迷が公募増資を通じた物件取得の制約となり得
る。実際、2025 年は、投資口価格が 1 口当たりの純資産価値（NAV）を下回る銘柄が多く、公募増
資が低調だった。一方、私募ファンドは、投資家の意向に応じた投資対象・運用戦略を設定して
出資を募る仕組みであり、上場市場とは異なる経路で不動産投資資金を受け入れている。こうし
た違いが、近年の資産拡大ペースの差に関係している可能性がある。 
本稿では、私募ファンドの仕組みや運用戦略を整理し、REITとの役割の違いを踏まえて、今後
の注目点を示す。

2．私募ファンドの仕組みと資金の出し手 
私募ファンドは、特定の投資家から集めた出資金と金融機関からの借入金等を用いて、不動産
を取得・運用する仕組みである。代表的な形態には、合同会社が匿名組合出資を受けるGK-TKスキ
ーム（資料2 左）や、特定目的会社を用いるTMKスキーム（資料2 右）がある。運用会社（アセッ
トマネジメント会社）が投資対象や運用方針に応じて物件の取得・管理・売却等を行う。

資料 2 私募ファンドの仕組みの概要 
<GK-TKスキームの例>    <TMKスキームの例>

（注）匿名組合出資は、合同会社が行う事業に出資し、その利益の分配を受ける契約に基づく出資。優先出資は、TMKの特定出
資に対し、利益配当又は残余財産の分配で優先する出資であり、借入金・社債より優先するものではない。特定出資等は図示を
省略。 
（出所）第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

GK-TKスキームでは、合同会社が投資家からの匿名組合出資と借入金により、通常、不動産信託
受益権を取得する。不動産自体は信託銀行等が所有し、合同会社はその収益等を受け取る。一
方、TMKスキームでは、特定目的会社が優先出資や借入金・社債等によって資金を調達し、不動産
を直接所有するか、不動産信託受益権を保有する。

### Page 3

3/4 
私募ファンドは、こうした法的な仕組みだけでなく、運用戦略や投資目的も多様である。資料 3
では、その代表例を整理する。

資料 3 私募ファンドの主な運用戦略・目的

（注）代表例を示したものであり、各区分は必ずしも相互に排他的ではない。 
（出所）第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

私募ファンドに出資する投資家としては、国内外の年金基金、保険会社、金融機関、政府系フ
ァンド、事業会社等がある。 
こうした資金を受け入れるファンドの運用戦略は、資料 3 に示したように、安定した賃料収入
を重視するものから、開発等による価値向上を目指すものまで幅広い。このように私募ファンド
は、単にJ-REITより流動性が低い投資商品というだけでなく、投資対象、保有期間、開発・改修
の有無等に応じた多様な投資機会を提供するものといえる。 
流動性の低さは投資上の制約であるが、長期資金を持つ投資家にとっては、それだけで投資対
象から外れるわけではない。投資対象や運用戦略を選択し、専門的な運用を通じて収益を獲得す
る手段として、私募ファンドが活用されている。

3．REITとの役割の違いと今後の注目点 
J-REITや私募リートが主として不動産の継続的な保有・運用による賃料収入を重視するのに対
し、私募ファンドには、開発や改修による価値向上と、その後の売却を組み合わせるものもあ
る。長期保有型も含む多様な戦略の中で、開発・価値向上段階のリスクを引き受ける資金の受け
皿となっている点に、私募ファンドの役割の一つがある。 
私募ファンドのうち、あらかじめ運用期間を定める有期ファンドでは、物件売却による資金回
収を予定する場合、その時期も運用計画に組み込まれる。契約等に基づいて運用期間を延長でき
る場合もあるが、延長の可否・条件や、必要に応じた借換えへの対応を考慮する必要がある。こ
のため、運用期限を設けないJ-REITや私募リートとは異なり、想定した時期・価格で売却できる
かに加え、売却を延期する場合の条件にも注意が必要である。 
また、私募ファンドの中には、将来のREIT等への売却を想定して物件を一時的に保有する「ブ
リッジファンド」もある。REITへの売却を想定するこうしたファンドでは、売却先となるREITの
取得余力も重要となる。このため、私募ファンドの拡大を、REITに代わる投資先の成長とのみ捉
えるのは十分ではない。開発や価値向上を担い、REIT等の長期保有主体に物件を供給する補完的

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
な役割にも目を向ける必要がある。 
今後は、私募ファンドの資金調達環境に加え、運用戦略の実行可能性や売却先となるREIT等の
取得余力にも着目し、市場拡大の持続性を見極めていくことが重要である。

十一月 09, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 連接器 ‧ 台灣

凡甲 
(3526.TW/3526 TT)

伺服器業務帶動 2027年 EPS成長，兼具高殖利率題材 
重要訊息 
3Q26 EPS 4.86 元，雖季增 12%、年增 19%，但因獲利率較低及匯損影響
而不如預期。4Q26-2027 年前景依然正向，動能自伺服器與電動車業務。 
評論及分析 
3Q26 EPS 不如預期。3Q26 EPS 為 4.86 元，略低於凱基預估，主因毛利率
及營利率分別為 48.4% 及 31.8%，均低於預期。公司指出，利潤率疲弱主
要受到銅成本上升（占銷貨成本 50-60%）及人民幣升值影響。3Q26 產品
組合有利於毛利率季增：伺服器營收比重小幅升至 57-58%，電動車小幅增
至 34-35%，但筆電降至 10% 以下，對比 2Q26 分別為 56-57%、32-
33% 及約 11%。加上約 3,300 萬元匯損，3Q26 EPS 低於預期，但累計 1-
3Q26 EPS 為 13.90 元，年增 13%。 
4Q26 和 2027 年營收成長動能仍來自伺服器與電動車應用。公司預期伺服
器及電動車營收成長將持續為 4Q26 及 2027 年營收的主要驅動。因季節性
因素有利，公司指引 4Q26 伺服器與汽車應用營收季增，但筆電營收季減。
其主要電動車客戶比亞迪 (中) 1H26 需求疲弱，隨 3Q26 後銷售回升，前 
10 月電動車累計出貨 362 萬輛，其中 10 月單月出貨約 49 萬輛，預估
2026 年電動車出貨量將與去年持平約 460 萬輛，因此預期凡甲 2026 年汽
車應用營收將低於我們之前的預估，但仍年增約 20%，而伺服器營收則年
增 35-40%。考量營收組合調整及毛利率預估修正，凱基預估 4Q26 營收季
增 2%，但下修 2026 年 EPS 預估至 19.15 元。展望 2027 年，公司並未提
供具體預算指引，但預期伺服器與電動車應用營收將持續推升整體營收及獲
利成長，受惠於穩健的 AI 伺服器高功率需求、IT 機櫃用電量提升，以及中
國電動車需求成長。儘管筆電營收持續下滑，預估 2027 年伺服器及汽車相
關營收將年增雙位數。公司並切入伺服器與汽車應用的儲能系統 (ESS) 產品
市場，如電動車電池組、附電動車充電器的 ESS，以及 BBU 相關連接器。預
期 2027 年伺服器及電動車營收占比將分別升至約 60% 與 32-33%，若銅
成本趨穩，將可維持利潤率，反映營收及毛利率預估修正，下修 2027 年 
EPS 預估至 24.43 元，年增 29%。公司近期宣布 25% 減資，將推升 2027 
年 EPS，但持續維持盈餘全數配發現金股利，現金殖利率持續豐厚。 
投資建議 
我們維持凡甲「增加持股」評等，考量伺服器相關需求成長，且現金殖利率
在 9%以上的高水準。我們將目標價由 380 調整為 325 元，相當於 2027 年
完全稀釋後平均每股盈餘的 15 倍。 
投資風險 
銅價上漲延續、伺服器與 EV 需求疲弱。

台灣 凡甲

十一月 09, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 3

圖 5: 公司概況  圖 6: 伺服器、車用為主要營收貢獻產品  
凡甲成立於 1988 年，在筆電以及伺服器連接器和網路線等產
品為一領先業者。2025 年公司伺服器營收占比約為 51%、筆
電 15%、車用 34%。凡甲致力於擴展非筆電產品的連接器營
收占比，包括伺服器、電動車、IPC 以及網路連接器等。凡甲
亦欲提升其高端伺服器營收占比，包括高功率、高速連接器，
以及可折彎高速線(F Cable)。公司目前亦供應電池管理系統連
接器(BMS)給中國的比亞迪以及寧德時代。

8210.TT 勤誠 
 缺料、量產延遲 衝擊 3Q26 獲利

[法說會更新]   
   公司簡介 
勤誠成立於 1983 年，主力業務為電腦週邊產品，包含伺服器機殼、電腦機殼、工業
電腦機殼之研發設計與銷售，並且逐步拓展資料中心與產業解決方案新事業。與 EMS
公司協同提供 OEM、ODM、JDM 服務。    
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
區間操作 650 
【前次投資建議】買進，1,110 
【大盤指數/股價】39702 / 546

➢ 缺料及量產進度不順，3Q26 營收、獲利皆衰退  
勤誠 3Q26 營收 74.0 億元，QoQ-5.45%，YoY+30.55%，毛利率 25.8%，
營益率 18.6%，EPS 8.06 元。3Q26 營收、毛利率、淨利率、EPS 皆較
上季衰退，主要原因為：大客戶 3Q26 零組件缺料、原定 9~10 月量產
的新專案進度延遲(勤誠目前正在調整製程)、客戶 1H26 拉貨積極 3Q26
則較為放緩，以上因素使公司 3Q26 營收、獲利都低於投研部預期。也
因為專案季度延遲，勤誠 3Q26 存貨較 2Q26 增加，公司表示會在 4Q26
進行庫存去化。 
➢ 4Q26 積極推進新專案量產  
新專案順利導入為首要目標，希望營收重返成長軌道，並降低庫存。勤
誠 10 月營收 23.1 億元 MoM-6.8%，YoY+15.8%，營收月減，也是受
新專案導入進度影響，公司表示，大客戶訂單量沒有減少，未來會強化
新品導入階段的製程驗證與跨部門協作，來解決延遲問題。投研部預估
勤誠 4Q26 營收 84.3 億元，QoQ+13.9%，YoY+25.0%，毛利率 26.9%，
營益率 19.0%，EPS 9.48 元。 
關於 2027 年 AI 市況，勤誠認為，近期美國期中選舉民主黨大勝(參議
院、眾議院皆過半，德州州長選舉亦獲勝)、CSP 自由現金流不足、CSP
對 AI 投資的成本回收效率問題，可能會使不同的客戶，在 AI 布局的路
線上出現階段性調整，但 AI 中長期需求是持續存在的，公司與大客戶在
美國廠區的建置繼續進行。 
➢ 建議區間操作，目標價 650 元  
勤誠 3Q26 營收、獲利不如預期，4Q26 力拼新產品盡快量產出貨，投
研部認為其營收、獲利可小幅回升。2H26 勤誠面臨客戶缺料、新專案量
產進度不順利的挑戰，加上美國期中選舉，主張加強監管 AI 的民主黨大
勝，可能不利 AI DC 建設，導致 AI 族群集體 PE 評價下修。勤誠歷史 PE
為 8X~30X，近期 AI 市場變數多，且公司新專案仍在調整製程，給予區
間操作評等，目標價 650 元(2027F PE 13X)。由於已偏評價下緣，建議
盤勢止穩、量產進度改善後可偏多操作。

缺料及量產進度不順，3Q26 營收、獲利皆衰退 
勤誠 3Q26 營收 74.0 億元，QoQ-5.45%，YoY+30.55%，毛利率 25.8%，營益率
18.6%，EPS 8.06 元。3Q26 營收、毛利率、淨利率、EPS 皆較上季衰退，主要原因
為：大客戶 3Q26 零組件缺料、原定 9~10 月量產的新專案進度延遲(勤誠目前正在調
整製程)、客戶 1H26 拉貨積極 3Q26 則較為放緩，以上因素使公司 3Q26 營收、獲利
都低於投研部預期。也因為專案季度延遲，勤誠 3Q26 存貨較 2Q26 增加，公司表示
會在 4Q26 進行庫存去化。

圖 1：勤誠產品組合

資料來源：勤誠 
4Q26 積極推進新專案量產 
勤誠 3Q26 專案進度不如預期，針對 4Q26 的營運策略，公司以既有專案穩定交付、
新專案順利導入為首要目標，希望營收重返成長軌道，並降低庫存。勤誠 10 月營收
23.1 億元 MoM-6.8%，YoY+15.8%，營收月減，也是受新專案導入進度影響，公司
表示，大客戶訂單量沒有減少，未來會強化新品導入階段的製程驗證與跨部門協作，
來解決延遲問題。勤誠會透過跨平台(NVIDIA/AMD/ASIC)、跨客戶(CSP/Neo-cloud/
企業/ODM)、跨產品(從伺服器機殼跨足機櫃)的策略來提升營運動能。投研部預估勤
誠 4Q26 營收 84.3 億元，QoQ+13.9%，YoY+25.0%，毛利率 26.9%，營益率 19.0%，
EPS 9.48 元。 
關於 2027 年 AI 市況，勤誠認為，近期美國期中選舉民主黨大勝(參議院、眾議院皆
過半，德州州長選舉亦獲勝)、CSP 自由現金流不足、CSP 對 AI 投資的成本回收效率
問題，可能會使不同的客戶，在 AI 布局的路線上出現階段性調整，但 AI 中長期需求
是持續存在的，公司與大客戶在美國廠區的建置繼續進行。  
昆山機櫃產線 3Q26 已投產 
勤誠總部位於新莊，生產基地位於中國(昆山/東莞)、台灣(樹林/嘉義)、馬來西亞，2026
年 CAPEX 預計 25~30 億台幣，預期 2027 年美國(德州)、越南會有新產能開出： 
1)昆山廠新增的機櫃產線 3Q26 已經投產，勤誠預期機櫃產品線營收將由 2026 年的
10%~15%，上升至 2027 年的 20%。 
2)2026 年 5 月正式簽約美國德州達拉斯量產廠區的興建計畫，德州廠預計 3Q27 開
始量產，預計 70%產能用於機櫃生產與組裝。

3)馬來西亞廠 3Q26 已進入試產，正處於產能爬坡階段，預計 1Q27 舉行正式開幕儀

4)2026 年 8 月決議設立越南子公司，金額新台幣 6 億元內，由於已取得客戶訂單，
因此先以租賃的方式取得廠房，越南廠將是機櫃生產與組裝基地，目前產線建置中，
自動化程度會高於昆山，預計 1H27 投產。

Document 658: 央视网评：离岸信托个税申报超1600亿，不是“劫富”，是收旧账

Source PDF: all report pdf/央视网评：离岸信托个税申报超1600亿，不是“劫富”，是收旧账.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

⼀些超⾼净值⼈群的财富，根植于国内市场、⽣⻓于本⼟营商环境、受益于完整的产业链
与消费群体，其利润源头本就在境内，纳税义务就应该落在这⾥。不能⼀边持续享⽤国内
市场的发展红利，⼀边搭⼀层境外架构，把本该承担的责任切割出去。
摘要：
1600 多亿申报⼊库， 10 ⽉单⽉⼊库超 1200 亿、带动当⽉全国个税收⼊增⻓近 90% 。数字出来的
时候，有些⾃媒体炒作： “ 对富⼈加税了 ” ？
恰恰相反。这 1600 多亿的离岸信托个税谈不上开征新税，更谈不上对哪⼀群⼈定向加税，它是
把本该缴的税，收回来。
这件事背后，有三层思考。
第⼀，财富源⾃国内，凭什么纳税义务就 “ 离岸 ” 了？
离岸信托这⼀⾦融⼯具，可⽤来跨境配置、资产传承、⻛险隔离，这是市场经济框架下的合法
安排。我们不该将它污名化，也不能落⼊⽚⾯的仇富叙事。但合法不等于没规则约束。
个⼈所得税，遵循量能课税的原则，收⼊越⾼、负税能⼒越强，该承担相应税负。可执⾏中，
存在反差：普通⼈的⼯资，发薪时就要被⽤⼈单位依法代扣代缴个税；⽽部分⾼净值⼈群，借

助离岸信托隐匿境内资产、滞留境外收益，利⽤低税地区信息不透明规避纳税义务，造成税负
失衡，让⼈感受到税负不公。
⼀些超⾼净值⼈群的财富，根植于国内市场、⽣⻓于本⼟营商环境、受益于完整的产业链与消
费群体，其利润源头本就在境内，纳税义务就应该落在这⾥。不能⼀边持续享⽤国内市场的发
展红利，⼀边搭⼀层境外架构，把本该承担的责任切割出去。
第⼆，过去收不上来，现在怎么收得上来了？
现在，条件具备了。居⺠个⼈要就全球所得依法纳税，这是《个⼈所得税法》确⽴的原则，也
是国际上普遍采⽤的征管惯例。
随着国际税收合作不断推进， CRS （共同申报准则）⾦融账⼾⾃动信息交换已常态化运⾏，跨
境资产、信托实际持有⼈、收益流向等数据得以互通，此前征管的信息壁垒，由此被打通。
同时，国内也补⻬了离岸信托全⽣命周期的征税规则。在设⽴、存续、终⽌清算三个环节，按
“ 财产转让所得 ” 或 “ 利息、股息、红利所得 ” 分别计税，统⼀适⽤ 20% 税率；境外已经缴纳的税款
依法抵免，避免双重征税；确因资⾦安排有困难、按规定备案的，可在 5 年内分期缴纳。

制度层⾯，贯彻 “ 实质重于形式 ” 的判定原则：资产登记在他⼈名下，但实际由本⼈出资、⾏使控
制权的，视作本⼈将财产装⼊信托；对离岸信托所控制的境外实体，即便利润没有汇回境内，
对应的收益同样需要计税。程序上，设置 90 天过渡期，在窗⼝期内主动申报补缴税款，不予加
收滞纳⾦，先留出⾃我纠错的合规渠道，期满后再转⼊常态化征管。
⾄此，相关征管升级为⼀套看得懂、能执⾏的完整制度体系。
这也给整个财富管理⾏业提了个醒：财富规划应依法纳税。靠搭建境外架构规避纳税义务，依
托信息差、规则漏洞牟利的灰⾊⽣意，随着信息壁垒被打破，已经难以为继。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

8 条评论
第三，这 1600 亿，和普通⼈有什么关系？
很多⼈会觉得，离岸信托补税只是⾼净值群体的事，和⾃⼰距离太远。
这 1600 亿资⾦最终将⽤于教育、社保、县域医疗等⺠⽣公共服务，补的是公共财政本就该夯实
的底盘。如果⼀部分资本收益⻓期脱离征管约束，由此形成的财政缺⼝，实际上就要由全社会
共同分担。把该征收的税款⾜额收归国库，⺠⽣公共服务才有稳定可持续的保障。
这件事，同样关乎各类市场主体的平等竞争。⼀部分经营者，如果可以借助离岸架构规避应尽
的纳税义务，相当于拿到隐性成本优势，会挤压合规经营主体的⽣存空间。堵上利⽤境外架构
规避税负的制度漏洞，让境内外资产、不同市场主体在同⼀套财税约束下公平开展经营。
财富可以跨境流动，但纳税不能 “ 离岸 ” 。以清晰税制引导跨境财富合规发展，守住的不仅是国家

税收权益，更是全社会对公平的信⼼。
规则之下，没有谁的义务可以悬空，也没有谁的权益可以被漠视。
本⽂来源：央视⽹

Document 690: 思科Q1财季营收创十五年来最快增速，上调全年指引，AI相关收入料将翻倍｜财报见闻

Source PDF: all report pdf/思科Q1财季营收创十五年来最快增速，上调全年指引，AI相关收入料将翻倍｜财报见闻.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

思科第⼀财季营收 185 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 24% ，调整后每股收益 1.40 美元，均超市场预
期。公司将全年收⼊指引上调⾄ 748 亿⾄ 758 亿美元，并预计本财年 AI 基础设施收⼊达 90 亿
美元，较上年翻倍。 CEO 表⽰公司正处于多年期⽹络超级周期早期阶段。
摘要：
思科系统公司发布强劲季度财报，营收和利润均超市场预期，并⼤幅上调全年业绩指引，显⽰ AI
基础设施建设热潮正为这家⽹络设备巨头带来持续动能。
11 ⽉ 12 ⽇美股盘后，思科发布截⾄ 10 ⽉ 24 ⽇的 2027 财年第⼀财季报告，营收同⽐增⻓ 24% ⾄
185 亿美元，创下逾 15 年来最强增速，调整后每股收益1.40 美元，均超出分析师预期。
公司同步将本财年来⾃超⼤规模数据中⼼运营商的AI 相关收⼊预期上调⾄ 90 亿美元，较上⼀财
年翻逾⼀倍。⾸席执⾏官 Chuck Robbins 表⽰：
不过，市场并未完全追⾼。思科今年以来股价累计上涨约 35% ，⼤幅跑赢同期纳斯达克指数约
9% 的涨幅。财报发布后，思科股价在盘后交易中⼀度涨 6% ，随后回落转跌约 1% 。

收⼊和利润全⾯超预期，创下多年最快增⻓节奏
我们正处于⼀个多年期⽹络超级周期的极早期阶段，各类机构正转向思科，借助我们的技术和专
业能⼒，为智能体 AI 时代扩展、保障并优化其运营环境。

### Page 2

从核⼼财务指标看，思科这⼀季度表现相当强劲。
第⼀财季收⼊为 185 亿⾄ 185.2 亿美元，同⽐增⻓约 24% ⾄ 24.4% ，⾼于市场普遍预期的约 181 亿
美元。调整后每股收益为 1.40 美元，⾼于市场预期的 1.32 ⾄ 1.33 美元，也较去年同期的 1.00 美元
明显增⻓。
公司管理层称，这是思科超过 15 年来收⼊和⾮ GAAP 每股收益增速最强劲的季度之⼀。对于⼀
家成熟⽹络设备巨头⽽⾔， 20% 以上的收⼊增⻓并不常⻅，这也是市场重新评估思科 AI 受益程度
的重要原因。
盈利能⼒同样稳健。思科当季运营利润率达到 30.2% ，⾼于去年同期的 22.6% ；调整后运营利润
率约为 35.2% ，与去年同期⼤体持平。⾃由现⾦流率为 20.8% ，也⾼于去年同期的 19.4% 。
这意味着，思科本季度收⼊增⻓并⾮完全依靠低⽑利扩张换来的规模增⻓，⽽是在保持较好利
润率的情况下实现了加速。
全年指引上调， AI 与企业⽹络更新共同⽀撑需求
指引是本次财报的另⼀⼤亮点。

思科预计第⼆财季收⼊将在 180 亿⾄ 182 亿美元之间，中值约 181 亿美元，⾼于分析师预期的约
178 亿⾄ 179 亿美元。公司预计第⼆财季调整后每股收益为 1.34 ⾄ 1.36 美元，也明显⾼于市场预
期的约 1.23 美元。
全年维度看，思科将本财年收⼊指引上调⾄ 748 亿⾄ 758 亿美元，中值约 753 亿美元，较此前约
728 亿美元的中值明显提升。调整后每股收益指引中值升⾄约 5.50 美元，较此前指引上调约
8.3% 。

### Page 3

推动指引上调的核⼼因素主要有两点：
换⾔之，思科⽬前并不只是押注 AI 超⼤规模客⼾，同时也受益于传统企业客⼾的资本开⽀回暖。
AI 基础设施收⼊预计达 90 亿美元，思科试图补上 AI 早期缺位
思科过去并不是 AI 热潮中最早被追捧的核⼼标的。相⽐英伟达、博通等公司，思科在⽣成式 AI 早
期阶段的市场叙事中存在感并不突出。但这份财报显⽰，公司正在加速切⼊ AI 基础设施链条。
公司预计，本财年来⾃超⼤规模云⼚商的 AI 基础设施收⼊将达到 90 亿美元，较上⼀财年翻倍以
上。这个数字是市场关注的焦点，因为它意味着思科已开始在 AI 数据中⼼建设中获得更⼤份额。
AI 基础设施并不仅仅需要 GPU 。随着模型规模扩⼤和推理需求增加，数据中⼼内部和数据中⼼
之间的⽹络能⼒变得越来越关键，包括交换机、路由器、光连接、安全产品和管理软件等。思
科的机会正是在这⼀环节。
CEO Robbins 在财报中强调，企业正在转向思科，借助其技术和经验来扩展、保护并优化⾯
向 “agentic AI era” 的环境。

订单增速放缓， AI 需求 “ 块状化 ” 成为最⼤短期变量
尽管业绩和指引强劲，但投资者反应并未过度乐观，⼀个重要原因是订单数据出现降温。
本季度思科产品订单同⽐增⻓ 21% ，仍是较⾼增速，但低于上⼀季度 35% 的增幅。财报电话会
上， UBS 分析师特别提到，来⾃超⼤规模云⼚商的 AI 订单出现⼩幅下滑。
⼀是 AI 数据中⼼建设带来的⽹络设备需求。 AI 训练和推理集群对⾼速互联、低延迟交换、安
全连接和流量管理提出更⾼要求，思科正在将其传统⽹络优势延伸⾄ AI 基础设施市场。
⼆是企业客⼾的⽹络更新周期。分析师指出，企业⽹络设备存在多年更新周期，尤其在安
全、云连接、混合办公和 AI 应⽤部署加速的背景下，企业对⽹络架构升级的需求正在回升。

### Page 4

Robbins 对此回应称， AI 订单本⾝会呈现 “lumpy” 特征，也就是季度之间波动较⼤：
这也是理解思科 AI 业务的关键。 AI 基础设施订单往往与⼤型云⼚商的数据中⼼建设节奏、资本开
⽀批准、芯⽚供应、机房交付和电⼒资源相关，因此不⼀定每个季度都稳定增⻓。
单季订单放缓不意味着需求反转，但会影响市场对短期增⻓斜率的判断。分析认为对于已经上
涨超过三成的思科股价⽽⾔，市场显然需要看到 AI 订单的持续兑现，才能继续给出更⾼估值。
股价年内⼤涨后，竞争和外部约束正在升温
思科股价今年以来已上涨约 32% ⾄ 35% ，显著跑赢纳斯达克。财报公布前，市场已经部分计⼊
了 AI ⽹络设备需求改善、企业⽹络更新周期以及全年指引上调的预期。
虽然思科在⽹络设备领域仍是全球⻰头，但 AI 基础设施市场竞争正在加剧。博通、 HPE 等公司
都在争夺 AI 数据中⼼⽹络相关机会，云⼚商⾃⾝也在推动定制化硬件和⾃研架构，这可能对传统
设备供应商的议价能⼒形成压⼒。
与此同时，数据中⼼建设也⾯临越来越多外部约束，包括电⼒供应、⼟地审批、社区反对和监

管压⼒。分析认为美国中期选举释放出对数据中⼼项⽬反弹升温的信号，这可能影响部分项⽬
推进节奏。
有些季度会很⼤，有些季度会⼩⼀些。

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

看的，是这场 “ ⽹络超级周期 ” 能否从单季度超预期，转化为连续多个财年的可持续增⻓。

Document 733: 米中間選挙で民主党が大勝 ～出口調査からみる共和党の敗因～

Source PDF: all report pdf/米中間選挙で民主党が大勝 ～出口調査からみる共和党の敗因～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

US Trends

2026年 11月 4日

1/3 
米中間選挙で民主党が大勝 
～出口調査からみる共和党の敗因～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  前田 和馬 
（℡：050-5473-3888）

11 月3 日に投開票を迎えた米国中間選挙において、民主党が上下院の過半数獲得を確実とし
た。New York Timesの4 日午前 3 時（米東部時間）時点の予測では、民主党の議席数は上院で 55
（選挙前：47）、下院で257（同 214）と大幅に議席を伸ばした見込みだ。CNNによる投票者調査
（事前調査と出口調査による集計：Voter polls）に基づくと、今回の選挙結果の背景として下記
3 点を指摘できる。なお、2024 年大統領選との比較に関してはサンプル規模など調査手法が異な
る点に留意が必要である。

① 若者や中南米系が共和党から離反 
 共和党は白人男性層に支持が多い一方、女性や若者は相対的に民主党を支持する傾向が強い。
2024 年大統領選と比べると、今回の中間選挙ではあらゆる属性で民主党の得票率が伸びており、
特に 18～29 歳の若者層や中南米系で上昇幅が大きい（図表 1）。 これらの有権者層は所得が相対的
に低く、住宅所有率も低いため、物価高に対するトランプ政権への不満が投票行動に反映されや
すかったとみられる。

② 物価高に対する不満 
「生活費の問題が投票に重要（主要）な影響を与えた」との回答は 89%に達し、経済や物価高の
問題が選挙における最大の争点の一つであった。なお、生活費以外の投票要因では政府の汚職が
90%に達し、イラン戦争は 74%、移民取締政策が73%、AI・データセンターが 56%と続く。なお、汚
職への高い回答割合は物価高への不満によって増長された可能性があり、2028 年大統領選でもこ
れが重要な争点となるかは不透明感が残る。 
経済運営を巡って、2024 年大統領選の際には 53%の有権者が「トランプ氏の経済運営の方が信
頼できる」と回答するなど（民主党・ハリス氏は 46%）、トランプ大統領の再選の背景には生活改
善への強い期待があった。一方、今回の中間選挙では「トランプ政策で経済環境が悪化した」と
の回答が65%に達するなど、高関税政策による輸入物価の上昇やイラン戦争によるガソリン価格高
騰への強い不満がみられた。

### Page 2

2/3 
③ 消去法的な民主党への投票 
「民主党を好ましい」とみる有権者は 47%（共和党：同 41%）と、「好ましくない」の 53%を下回
っている。また、全有権者の 18%は「民主党をやや好ましくない」と回答したものの、このうち
46%が民主党候補に投票している。すなわち、全有権者の8%程度は消去法的に民主党候補を選んだ
様子がみてとれる。加えて、全有権者の12%は「社会主義をやや好ましくない」と回答した一方、
このうち71%は民主党候補に投票している。このため、2028 年大統領選までに民主党が左派色を
強める場合、こうした投票者層は再び共和党へ投票先を変更する可能性がある。

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3

・今後の政策見通し 
 民主党は上下院で勝利したものの、トランプ大統領は議会が可決した法案への拒否権を有して
いる。拒否権を覆すには上下両院でそれぞれ 3 分の 2 以上の賛成が必要であり、共和党議員の大
幅な造反がなければ実現は難しいだろう。一方、トランプ政権は議会との対立が強まるなか、大
統領令を活用すると同時に、大統領権限が強い外交や関税政策でのレガシー作りを目指すとみら
れる。イラン戦争は議会による国防予算への権限、関税政策は司法判断がどの程度の歯止めにな
るかが注目される。他方、AI規制は連邦レベルでの規制案が合意に達するか不透明であり、州・
地方政府レベルでのデータセンター建設規制等が続く可能性がある（詳細は 2026 年 10 月28 日付
け「米国：民主党勝利で政策は変わるのか？」）。

米国 10 月 ISM 製造業指数は予想を上振れ、仕入価格は高止まり 
～AI・防衛等の需要が底上げ、インフレ懸念に伴う米金利上昇・ドル強含みへ～ 
 経済調査部 主任エコノミスト  桂畑 誠治 
（℡：050-5474-7493）

要旨  
・10 月のISM製造業景気指数は 55.5（前月比＋1.0 ポイント）と市場予想（54.5）を上回り、
10 カ月連続で景気拡大・縮小の分岐点である 50 を上回った。好調なAI需要に加えて、関税引き
上げ前の前倒し発注や地政学リスクを背景とした在庫再構築の動きが、景気を力強く底上げして
いる。 
・AIや防衛など特需分野の急成長が牽引する一方、コスト高や政策警戒感に悩む基礎産業との
「二極化」が鮮明化している。仕入価格指数は 75.2 と高水準で推移し、強いコスト増圧力が持
続している。 
・FRBの追加利上げ観測が高まるなか、製造業の堅調さと価格高止まりはFRBのタカ派スタンス
（金融引き締め継続）を強力に補強する。米長期金利の上昇とドル高の進行が予想される。

1．総合指数は 上昇 し高水準 を維持  
2026 年 10 月のISM製造業景気指数（季節調整値）は 55.5（前月：54.5）と、市場予想中央値
（54.5）を上回り前月比＋1.0 ポイント上昇した。拡大・縮小の分岐点である 50 を 10 カ月連続で
上回り、米製造業の堅調な拡大基調が維持されている。 
足元では、トランプ政権の通商政策に伴う不確実性やコスト増が生産活動の抑制要因となって
いるものの、堅調なAI投資などの国内需要を背景とした在庫補充に加え、関税引き上げや中東情
勢を見越した顧客側の前倒し発注が指数全体を下支えした。

ＩＳＭ製造業景気指数の推移
（注）シャドー部は景気後退期。
(出所)ＩＳＭより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

2．調査コメントにみる 「業種間の二極化」 と地政学 ・政策 リスク  
パネリストのコメントからは、インフレや政策リスクによるコスト圧力を共通課題としつつ
も、業種ごとに「特需に沸く分野」と「停滞・様子見の分野」への二極化が浮き彫りとなってい
る。

●特需・拡大分野（機械、その他製造業等）： 
AI・データセンター投資、半導体、防衛、自治体インフラ（水関連）などの爆発的な成長に牽
引され、旺盛な需要が継続している。一方で、急激な需要増がサプライチェーンをひっ迫させて
おり、部品のリードタイムの深刻な長期化（4 週間から 50 週間への伸長）や価格高騰、納期クレ
ームなどの課題が浮上している。

●停滞・静観分野（輸送機器、コンピュータ・電子製品等）： 
輸送機器では価格高騰に対する顧客の警戒感が強く、年末の予算消化特需の消失や貨物需要の
低下が目立っている。電子製品でも中東・東欧等の地政学リスクや研究資金への懸念から、新規
装置の導入を見送り既存ツールの最適化に留める「静観モード」が広がっている。

●コスト高・基礎産業（化学製品、食品・飲料・タバコ、金属製品、一次金属等）： 
想定を超えるコスト増（6％以上）、軽油高、トラック運転手不足、対カナダ関税の影響が直撃
している。また、ワシントンの経済政策に対する不透明感から顧客が超短期契約へシフトしてお
り、長期的な経営予測を一層困難にさせている。

ＩＳＭ景気指数の推移

（注）シャドー部は景気後退期(出所)ＩＳＭより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成
3．主要な 構成項目 が拡大圏 を維持  
10 月の総合景気指数を項目別にみると、雇用の鈍化が押し下げ要因となったものの、生産、新
規受注、在庫、サプライヤーの入荷遅延の強まりが全体を押し上げた。一方、価格指数は前月よ
り若干低下したものの依然として 75 超の極めて高い水準にあり、製造業におけるコスト増圧力の
根強さを示している。 
●総合指数への寄与度（前月比＋1.0 ポイント）  
雇用（▲0.06 ポイント）が押し下げに寄与した一方、在庫（＋0.56 ポイント）、生産（＋0.46
ポイント）、新規受注（＋0.04 ポイント）、入荷遅延（＋0.02 ポイント）のプラス寄与が押し上げ
に貢献した（四捨五入の影響を含む）。新規受注、生産、雇用、在庫、入荷遅延はいずれも 50 を
超える拡大圏を維持している。

●主要項目の詳細動向 
・新規受注（55.5/前月：55.3）：前月から小幅上昇し、高い水準を維持して需要の堅調さを示して
いる。ただし、AI 関連需要に加えて、米国・イラン交渉の停滞に伴う価格上昇や供給懸念に先んじ
た前倒し発注が下支え要因となっている面もある。全 18 業種中 11 業種で拡大、縮小は 4 業種。

・生産（59.0/前月：56.7）：高水準に上昇し、生産活動の強まりを示している。拡大は 11 業種、縮
小は 3 業種。新規受注および受注残が拡大水準を維持していることから、生産拡大の持続性は高い
とみられる。

・雇用（52.4/前月：52.7）：前月から低下したものの、拡大圏を維持した。労働市場の緩やかな改
善を示唆する一方、拡大 8 業種に対し縮小が 5 業種となった。マクロ環境としては、需要動向の不
透明感を背景に、企業がレイオフや採用凍結を通じた人員・人件費調整を優先する姿勢を継続して
いると考えられる。

ＩＳＭ景気指数の推移
在庫
入荷遅延
（注）シャドー部は景気後退期(出所)ＩＳＭより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成
・在庫（51.4/前月：48.6）：拡大圏へ上昇した。地政学的な不確実性の高まりから慎重な在庫管
理姿勢が続くものの、手元在庫の減少や顧客側の在庫不足感を背景に一定の積み増しが進んでい
る。なお、顧客在庫指数は低下傾向が続いており（適正水準を下回る状態）、将来的な需要回復期
における在庫の再積み増しが今後の生産を刺激する潜在的な下支え要因となる。

・入荷遅延（59.1/前月：59.0）：小幅に上昇し、依然として 50 を大きく上回っている。港湾での
停滞やトラック輸送の制約、悪天候に加え、イランを含む中東情勢の混乱が、サプライヤーの納
入期間を長期化させる主因となっている。

・貿易関連：サブ項目をみると、新規輸出受注が 51.2（前月：50.9）、輸入指数は 52.9（前月：
51.0）とそれぞれ拡大圏で上昇した。輸入は、サプライチェーン寸断への懸念から物資を前倒し
で確保する動きにも支えられ高水準を維持した。

4．業種 別の広がり （12 業種 が拡大 ） 
10 月に総合指数が拡大した業種は、全 18 業種のうち、プラスチック・ゴム製品、電気機器・電
化製品・電気部品、一般機械、コンピュータ・電子製品、繊維、一次金属、輸送機器、加工金
属、家具・同関連製品、化学製品、その他製造業、食品・飲料・タバコ製品の 12 業種（前月：12
業種）となった。 
主要 6 業種のうち拡大した業種は、石油・石炭製品を除く 5 業種（コンピュータ・電子製品、
一般機械、輸送機器、化学製品、食品・飲料・タバコ製品）である。一方、縮小した業種は印

ＩＳＭ景気指数の推移
仕入価格
入荷遅延
（注）シャドー部は景気後退期(出所)ＩＳＭより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成
刷・関連支援活動、紙製品、非金属鉱物製品の 3 業種（前月：2 業種）に増加した。なお、石油・
石炭製品、木材製品、アパレル・皮革製品は前月から横ばいとなっている。

5．高止まりする 仕入価格 と供給不足  
インフレ動向を示す仕入価格指数は、75.2（前月：77.9）と小幅低下したものの、依然として
極めて高い水準にとどまっている。10 月に物価上昇を報告した回答者の割合は 53.1％となり、9
月の 58.6％から低下したものの、製造業における強いコスト増圧力が持続していることを裏付け
ている。鉄鋼やアルミニウム価格の上昇、関税、中東情勢の緊迫化を受けた石油製品の高騰が仕
入価格指数を押し上げている。価格上昇を報告した業種は 16 業種（前月：16 業種）に及び、価格
下落を報告した業種はゼロであった。  
商品別では、アルミニウム、銅、鉄鋼・同製品、電子・メモリ部品、半導体、プリント基板、
熱間圧延鋼、樹脂、ステンレス鋼、貨物（運賃）、燃料、石油製品、ディーゼル燃料、段ボール製
品など広範囲な品目で価格上昇が確認された。また、供給不足品目として電子部品、電気部品、
メモリ、プリント基板、集積回路といったAI関連のほか、鉄鋼、銅、アルミニウム製品が挙げら
れ、トランプ政権の対外・通商政策に伴う供給下押し圧力が継続している。

6．先行き展望：短期的混乱と中期的な拡大基調  
今後の製造業の業況について、短期的には米国とイランの停戦合意の履行や和平交渉を巡る不
確実性が足かせとなるものの、一段のコスト上昇を警戒した需要の前倒しが当面の下支え要因に
なると見込まれる。 
中期的には、①半導体、医薬品、重要鉱物等への分野別関税によるインフレ波及が限定的とな
る公算が高いこと、②AI投資に加えて減税や給与所得・資産価格の上昇を背景に国内需要が堅調

を維持すること、③低水準にある顧客在庫が生産を誘発すること等に支えられ、製造業は 2027 年
前半にかけても拡大の勢いを保つ可能性が高いと考えられる。

7．FRB の政策 スタンスと 金融 市場 への影響  
9 月の利上げ実施に続き、FRBによる追加利上げ観測が市場で高まるなか、ISM製造業の強さ
（55.5）と仕入価格の高止まり（75.2）は、根強いインフレ圧力を実証した。このようなデータ
は、FRBがタカ派スタンス（引き締め継続）を維持し、追加利上げへ踏み切る強い正当性を与え
る。潜在成長率を超える景気拡大とインフレ圧力の再燃を背景に、FRBは 12 月FOMCで 25bpの追加
利上げを実施する見込みである。2027 年も堅調な経済と鈍いインフレ低下を背景に、政策金利は
高い水準で維持される可能性が高い。 
FRBのターミナルレート（最終到達金利）切り上げ観測や高金利環境の長期化が予想されるな
か、米 10 年債利回りには上昇圧力がかかりやすく、内外金利差の拡大を通じて為替市場ではドル
強含みの展開が予想される。
